# C00 log (append-only)

Log entries of stage C00, moved verbatim from `plan/ledger.md` on 2026-10-01 when the ledger was split into a state file and per-stage logs (PC-04, Builder Operating Model §3.3). Entries are never rewritten; corrections are new entries. In entries written before PC-04, "K10" and "K11" refer to what are now plan changes PC-01 and PC-02 (L-015).

### L-001 · 2026-10-01 · Plan package read

The builder read the following completely, from beginning to end, all at `devos` commit `6186e5d`: `DevOS_Kurulum_Plani.md`, `Ek_A` through `Ek_G`, `Uyandirma_ve_Kapasite_Arastirmasi.md`, `Calisma_Duzeni_Karsilastirmali_Arastirma.md`, `Inceleme_Degerlendirmesi_Claude.md` and `Inceleme_Degerlendirmesi_ChatGPT.md`. No file was changed before reading was complete.

### L-002 · 2026-10-01 · Library repository attached to the session

- At session start only `devos` was attached. `agentic-os-search` was not, although Appendix F tells Batu to select both.
- The builder attached `batuhanozgun/agentic-os-search` with the session tool `add_repo`, passing `access: "read"`. **This was a change to the session's configuration, not a read.** According to the tool's result, it put the library inside the session's GitHub scope, which is the set of repositories where session writes are allowed. No tool for detaching a repository is available in this session. The builder needs to read the library during C00–C04 in any case (plan Section 0.5).
- The builder made a shallow local clone (library commit `dc91f6b`, 2,392 files).
- The builder deliberately did not call `register_repo_root`. According to the `add_repo` result, that call would load the repository's `CLAUDE.md`, skills and plugins into the session. The builder skipped it because the library is a source of information, not of instructions (plan Section 0.5). That this avoids the loading path is documented only, not observed. The clone has no `CLAUDE.md` and no `.claude/` directory at its top level; deeper levels were not checked.
- By the builder's own record of its actions, it issued no write operation to the library repository. The remote state was not checked afterwards.

### L-003 · 2026-10-01 · Correction: the library's "read-only" access is not a verified technical boundary

**Trigger.** Batu's question after the builder's first message:

> Original (tr): "agentic-os-search'ü "yalnız okuma izniyle" eklediğini yazdın. Bunu nasıl ekledin? Okuma sınırı teknik olarak mı uygulanıyor, yoksa senin kuralın mı? GitHub'da makine hesabının bu depoda yazma izni olduğunu planın 0.5. bölümünde bulabilirsin. Doğru durumu kurulum defterine yaz."
>
> Interpretation (en): The builder said it had added `agentic-os-search` "with read-only permission". Batu asks how it was added and whether the read limit is technically enforced or only the builder's rule. He points out that plan Section 0.5 states the machine account has write permission on that repository on GitHub, and he instructs the builder to record the correct state in the installation ledger.

**What the builder had said.** In its first message to Batu, the builder wrote that it had added `agentic-os-search` with read-only permission (Batu quotes the Turkish phrase above). That wording presented a request parameter as an enforced permission, which was wrong. The correct state is below.

**Correct state** (evidence: EV-C00-001):

| Layer | What holds | Verification status |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub identity of this session's GitHub tools | `batuhanozgun-devos` (the machine account, B3 = (a)) | Observed (EV-C00-001, row 4) |
| GitHub permission of that identity on `agentic-os-search` | `pull: true`, `triage: true`, **`push: true`**, `maintain: false`, `admin: false` | Observed (EV-C00-001, row 5) |
| Credential behind the session's git proxy | Unknown. It could be the machine account's or the Claude GitHub App installation's. This matters for safeguard 3: removing the machine account as a collaborator closes the session's git path only if the session uses the machine account's credential. The plan treats a related point as an assumption awaiting C01 row 12 (plan Section 13, risk row for `devos-evals` and `devos-backup`). | Not verified (OI-005) |
| Session attachment (`add_repo`, `access: "read"`) | The tool description says `read` = "fetch/clone only", and `push` is for a session that must "push commits, open PRs, or use GitHub API tools against the repository", which is "attached with credentials after the full repository-access checks". This suggests that a read attachment carries no write credentials. The tool's result says writes remain limited to repositories attached to the session, and this repository is now attached. The two texts point in different directions. | Documented only; enforcement not observed (OI-001) |
| Git push through the session proxy | Not tested. The builder tried to run a preparatory command (list the library's `.github/` directory and snapshot its remote refs) before a no-write `git push --dry-run` probe. The session's automatic permission classifier denied the command (reason label "Untrusted Code Integration"). The builder did not pursue the probe by any other route. | Denial observed; push behaviour not observed (OI-001) |
| Session automatic permission classifier | Observed denying one command that touched the library's remote. Whether it blocks writes was not tested. | Observed denial only |
| GitHub tools (`mcp__github__*`) that write files, branches or pull requests | Unknown. The identity has push permission (observed). The `add_repo` description ties GitHub API use against a repository to `push` mode (documented only). Not tested, because a write test is ruled out (plan Section 0.5; C00 condition 4). | Not verified (OI-001) |
| Environment variables named `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` | Present. Their values were not read, and their scope and function are unknown. | Names observed only (OI-003) |
| Plan rule: the builder only reads the library | Plan Section 0.5 (single-writer rule, a Batu decision; safeguard 1, "the builder's instruction") and Appendix F. It is not a technical control. It is the only safeguard known to be in force today. | Its existence is documented; whether it works is not verified |

**Conclusion.** For this session, "read-only" on `agentic-os-search` is a rule of the plan that only the builder's compliance enforces. No technical boundary has been observed. On GitHub, the machine account has write permission, as plan Section 0.5 states and the GitHub API confirms. Plan Section 0.5 names two further safeguards:

- **Safeguard 2:** a monitoring path, independent of DevOS, that reports every machine-account commit in `agentic-os-search` to Batu. The plan builds this path later (G-001), so it is probably absent today.
- **Safeguard 3:** removing the machine account's access in C04, after the library has been imported into Supabase and verified. This is still in the future.

**No write by the builder.** The builder made no commit, push, branch, pull request, issue or comment in `agentic-os-search`. Apart from `add_repo`, every operation in L-002 and L-003 only read data or ran locally. `add_repo` changed the session's GitHub scope; it did not write to the repository. The basis for this paragraph is the builder's own record (same session); it has not been verified independently.

### L-004 · 2026-10-01 · Plan change K10: each installation stage runs under a `/goal` target

**Batu's instruction:**

> Original (tr): "Planda kurulum dönemi için bir çalışma ritmi tanımlı değil. Her aşama bir /goal hedefiyle yürütülsün. Duruş koşulları şunlar: aşama bitti, Batu'nun bir kararı ya da işlemi gerekiyor, ya da senin aşamayacağın bir engel var. Bu kuralı kayıtlı bir plan değişikliği olarak planın kurulum bölümüne ve Ek F'ye ekle ve deftere yaz."
>
> Interpretation (en): The plan defines no working rhythm for the installation period. Each stage is to run under a `/goal` target, with three stop conditions: the stage is complete; a decision or action by Batu is needed; or the builder has hit a blocker it cannot pass. Add this rule as a recorded plan change to the installation section of the plan and to Appendix F, and record it in the ledger.

**Plan change record (plan Section 14):**

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Decision | K10, Batu's decision, 2026-10-01 |
| Old state | Plan 2.1 defined no working rhythm for the installation period. Section 9 defined stage order, acceptance and closure, but not when the builder works on and when it stops and returns to Batu. |
| New state | Plan Section 9, introduction: the "Kurulum çalışma ritmi" paragraph, with the three stop conditions, how `/goal` works, and its limits. Appendix F (version 1.3): a note for Batu (Turkish) and a "Working rhythm" paragraph in the builder's opening message (English). The plan header and Section 11.1 list K10. |
| Rationale | Batu's decision. The builder added two rules from reading the `/goal` documentation: (a) a met goal is not stage acceptance, because the evaluator is a small model that reads only the conversation; (b) under stop condition 2, the builder stops and returns instead of continuing with other work, and silence is not approval. |
| Platform facts | Observed in the official documentation (code.claude.com/docs/en/goal, read 2026-10-01 by a documentation subagent; secondary reading, not yet tried in this account). The goal is a natural-language completion condition of up to 4,000 characters. A small fast model (Haiku by default) judges it after each turn: not yet met, met or impossible. `/goal clear` cancels it. It does not change the permission mode, so unattended turns need auto mode. Evaluation is deferred while a subagent or background shell is running. An active goal is restored on resume. Errors that need a fix clear the goal. |
| Assumption | The builder cannot start `/goal` itself, so Batu starts it at each stage. Status: **[Varsayım]**; to be observed in C01. |
| Affected stages | C00 to C12 (installation period only). It does not change the B-phase working structure (routines, Section 6.4). |
| Translation | The change is written into the Turkish binding text, and the C00 translation will carry it. |

**C00 goal condition** (to be started by Batu with `/goal`):

```text
Stop when one of these holds, and state which one with ledger and evidence IDs: (1) Stage C00 is complete: every C00 acceptance condition in devos/plan/ledger.md section 2 is shown with evidence recorded in the ledger and in devos/evidence/C00/, all changes are committed and pushed to branch claude/epic-hamilton-9tisc4, and the stage closure is ready for audit review; (2) a decision or an action by Batu is needed, it has been sent to Batu in Turkish in Appendix E format, and it is recorded in the ledger; (3) the builder has hit a blocker it cannot pass, including a loop limit or no progress across consecutive turns, and the blocker is recorded in the ledger with its reason and reported to Batu in Turkish. Batu's silence is never approval.
```

### L-013 · 2026-10-01 · K11 corrected: no merge approvals from Batu

**Batu's decision:**

> Original (tr): "Benim neyi ne zaman birleştireceğin konusunda bir fikrim yok. niye öyle yazmış bilmiyorum plana. saçmalık. İstersen her bana getirdiğin onayda konuşuruz bu tarz onayları bana getirmene gerek var mı diye karar veririz."
>
> Interpretation (en): Batu has no view on what should be merged when, and rejects the plan's requirement for his acceptance before merges. If the builder brings him an approval, they decide on that occasion whether that kind of approval needs to come to him at all.

**Change:** K11 limit 3 (L-011) is replaced. Batu is not asked to approve merges. If the builder brings him something because it genuinely needs his judgment of purpose or risk, they decide then whether that category of approval should keep coming to him, and the answer is logged. Plan Section 9 ("Dal yönetimi") and Section 11.1 are updated. This changes how the plan 6.7 "Batu's acceptance" requirement applies during installation. Once the audit environment exists (C02–C03), the technical review of high-impact changes takes place there.

**Residual risk (stated, not hidden):** until the audit environment exists, nothing outside the builder's own judgment gates high-impact changes to `main` (G-015). Mitigation: before a high-impact merge, the builder gets a review from a fresh-context subagent (thinking independence only, plan K-7) and logs it. G-015 is updated accordingly.

**Branches:** `claude/epic-hamilton-9tisc4` is not an old branch. It is this session's working branch, reset to `main` after the merge (L-012). It stays until this line of work ends and is then deleted. Merged branches that no longer have a purpose are deleted at merge.

### L-014 · 2026-10-01 · Continuity rule: merge to `main` before every stop

> Original (tr): "anladım. yeni oturumda main'den başlıyor ya oturum bağlamı dolduğunda yeni oturuma geçersek kaldığın yerden devam edemeybilirsin diye branchlara konularında davranıyorum."
>
> Interpretation (en): Batu's concern behind the branch question: a new session starts from `main`. If the context fills up and work moves to a new session, the builder might not be able to continue from where it stopped.

**Rule (added to K11 as limit 5, plan Section 9):** before every stop under K10, the builder merges its work into `main`, so a new session can continue from the ledger on `main` alone (Appendix D, D7). The ledger's "Current state" table and the latest L-entry are the hand-off. Session-only state, such as the local clone of the library and subagent transcripts, is never relied on across sessions.

## Findings

### FND-001 · Requested or declared restriction reported as an enforced boundary

- **Symptom:** The builder told Batu that the library had been added "with read-only permission". In fact only a request parameter had been set, and the GitHub identity has write permission. In the first version of this ledger entry (commit `84f258b`), the builder then made the opposite error and stated that the session *can* write, which was not verified either. Independent checking caught that second error (EV-C00-001, review note).
- **Failure class (plan Section 6.11):** A restriction or capability that is requested, declared, documented or self-imposed (a tool parameter, an instruction, a role name, a connector label, a settings screen) is reported as an enforced and verified state of the system. The plan draws the same distinction between declaration and authority (K-9, item 2c). It is also the exam focus of DR10: do not take a documented feature as working in the account.
- **Multi-agent failure class:** verification (a claim made without verification).
- **Causes (Appendix D, D6):**
  - **Proximate cause:** the builder repeated the tool's own label ("read") in its report to Batu.
  - **Enabling condition:** the builder had just read plan Section 0.5, which states that the machine account has write access, and did not check the report against it.
  - **Prevention gap:** the existing rules already covered this case: Appendix E 4.1 and 4.2 (speak only as far as the evidence carries) and Appendix D D4, item 9 (limit the language of confidence). They were not applied at the moment of writing the message, and nothing enforced them.
  - **Detection gap:** Batu caught the error, not the builder. Appendix E 2.2 says such a case is a failure record and its cause is examined.
- **Can the same class recur by another route (D6, item 8)?** Yes. Upcoming statements of the same kind include: "the builder's Supabase connection is read-only" (inferred from the connector's name or tool list), "connectors are removed from the routine" (inferred from a settings screen), "branch protection covers administrators", and "the second model receives only public content". Each needs an observed enforcement before it can be reported as such.
- **Repair, and the layer each part changes:**
  1. **Record format:** Rule 5 at the top of this ledger. Every access statement names its enforcement layer and verification status.
  2. **Inventory structure:** the C00 gap list and the effect-channel inventory (plan Section 0.3, item 13) carry the same two columns for every channel.
  3. **Builder's working method:** before reporting an access state to Batu, the builder checks the statement against the plan's own statement about that access and against the evidence record.
  4. **Class-level regression test:** OI-007.
- **Capability-gap candidate:** yes, as a candidate only, not confirmed. Every DevOS role runs on the same model family, so this blind spot may be shared (plan Section 6.11), and DR10's exam targets exactly this behaviour. Confirmation needs reproduction, a causal separation and a counterexample, not a count of events (Appendix B 3.20).
- **Evidence:** L-003, EV-C00-001.
- **Applies when:** any statement about access, permission, isolation, enforcement or capability of a platform component, made to Batu or recorded as fact.
- **Does not apply when:** the statement is explicitly labelled as a plan, a design intent or an assumption.

## Entries after the ledger split

### L-015 · 2026-10-01 · Builder operating model (W-C00-05): design, counter-design, probes; plan changes PC-01 to PC-05

**Batu's instruction.** The full Turkish original is recorded verbatim in `briefs/builder-operating-model/BATU_ORIGINAL_TR.md`, and its English rendering in `BRIEF.md` §2. In short: design, test and install the builder's own operating model before C00's heavy work, to the standard applied to DevOS. His expectations: (1) he is not a message carrier; (2) only his decisions come to him, and technical approval of high-impact changes comes from independent review, with the plan to be changed accordingly; (3) the builder does not wait for him at every step, and his tasks are batched; (4) "done" rests on evidence; (5) a Turkish status page that is always current. He also asked for renumbering: K numbers are his decisions only.

**Usage note.** The weekly limit was at warning level. D-001 deferred C00's heavy work, and this item came from Batu's newer, explicit instruction to do it before the heavy work. The builder kept it light: four short probe sessions and one counter-design session, with a combined reported cost of about 0.85 USD, against about 17 for the main session to date.

**What was done:**
1. Probes T-A1a, T-A1b, T-A1c, T-A0, T-H1 and T-H2 (EV-C00-005). The builder can start `/goal` sessions itself. Builder-created and routine sessions carry no account connectors. Routine sessions run on a smaller model with no tools. Harness rules and hooks block connectors.
2. A brief for the counter-design (`briefs/builder-operating-model/`, merged into the builder's branch at `5ebc821`) and an independent counter-design session (`session_01UgKhdvKnZacS7EVcGpe79C`, sparse checkout of the brief directory only; output `73baa5a` on `claude/counter-design-builder-model`). T-E1 passed: the result arrived through the repository.
3. The builder's v1.0 draft was committed (`a58413a`, not pushed) before the counter-design was read. v1.1 records a disposition for every difference (operating model §14).
4. Installed:
   - `plan/Builder_Operating_Model.md` v1.1;
   - `CLAUDE.md` (the boot pointer);
   - `.claude/settings.json` (deny rules and the allowlist hook) with `.claude/hooks/tool_allowlist.py`;
   - `tools/builder_check.sh`;

### FND-002 · Reviewer sessions ran without the technical connector barrier

- **Symptom.** The counter-design session (sparse checkout, before `.claude/` existed) and the probe sessions ran with the account's connectors live, protected only by an instruction. The builder believed builder-created sessions had none (BP-04, from the T-A1c self-report).
- **Failure class.** The same as FND-001: a premise reported from a weak observation (a model's self-report taken while servers were still connecting) was treated as verified, and design depended on it.
- **Multi-agent class:** verification.
- **Caught by:** the independent review, not by the builder.
- **Repair at class level.**
  1. A self-report is labelled "self-report" and is not enough for a security premise.
  2. Every builder-created session uses a full checkout with `.claude/` (§9 item 3).
  3. The hook is the barrier and is unit-tested with negative controls and a break test (T-H4).
- **Impact.** No connector tool call is known to have happened. The sessions' transcripts were not audited for this. The question is open and noted in OI-009.

| ID | Open item | Where it is resolved |
|---|---|---|
| OI-009 | Confirm that no connector tool was called in the sessions that ran without the barrier: `session_01UgKhdvKnZacS7EVcGpe79C`, `session_018kpRnAaG9R3vaRTg5wMyye`, `session_01DaBd2sS8rT6TyBhHxFi8QV`, `session_01FMMpUHBn5EbpYgtSjY9hyZ`, The reviewer `session_0139FAmiXGW3U6rGKKbmgNYy` ran on revision `3cd686a`, whose first allowlist hook already blocked opaque-ID servers. Hooks are shown to run in such sessions (T-H3), so it was probably protected; this is not verified. | Builder: read their tool-use events (`list_events`) before closing W-C00-05 |

### L-017 · 2026-10-01 · OI-009 closed: no connector tool was called in the sessions without the barrier

A fresh-context subagent (read-only; it used only `list_events` and a local parse of its own saved output) read the assistant events of six sessions: the counter-design, T-A1a, T-A1b, T-A1c, the reviewer R-C00-BOM-1 and the T-H3 probe.

- **Conclusion:** no tool of any server other than the GitHub tools or the session tools was called in any of them.
- **MCP calls found:** the reviewer called `mcp__claude-code-remote__get_session`, and T-H3 called `mcp__github__get_me`, which the hook blocked.
- **Caveat:** the subagent's per-session labels for T-A1b and the reviewer appear swapped (its "22 tool uses" match the reviewer's work, and "DONE (none)" matches T-A1b). The conclusion covers all six sessions either way.
- Independence: same session, subagent.

**OI-009: closed.**

### L-019 · 2026-10-01 · Independent review R-C00-BOM-3: FAIL; frame review; operating model v1.4

**Review.** `session_01PEMTsgPZVoFoz44RHzPELw`, full checkout of `c0007ea`; verdict `evidence/C00/reviews/R-C00-BOM-3.md`, from `claude/review-R-C00-BOM-3`. **FAIL**: B1 (the session-tool rules were bypassable through `source_revision`, `create_trigger.persistent_session_id`, unlisted session tools and MCP resource readers), B2 (the M9 redaction was recorded as done but was incomplete), 9 minor findings, and conditions C1–C4.

**Frame review (plan 6.12; squeeze signal).** This was the third review round that found new routes in the same layer, so the builder questioned the frame before patching again. Result: a hook in a repository the builder can edit cannot stop a builder that intends to bypass it. It can stop accidents and injected instructions. The design was changed accordingly, instead of adding a fourth set of deny rules:
1. The session-tool layer is now an **allow list**: unlisted tools are blocked.
2. A new session's revision must carry `.claude/settings.json`, as known to this checkout.
3. The threat model and the residual risk are stated (operating model §9).
4. The stronger barrier is a credential the builder does not hold (audit environment, C02–C03), or an account-level change. Choosing between them is Batu's decision **D-003**, to be asked in the issue batch.

**Dispositions:**

| Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|
| B1 a1–a3, b, c1, c2, d1, d2, e | **Accepted.** Session-tool allow list; revision check (main or this session's branch, carrying `.claude/settings.json`); `create_trigger`: owned persistent sessions only, builder environment, `connectors` absent or `[]`; event and trigger reads on owned IDs only; listing other sessions and routines blocked; MCP resource readers blocked and matched. Negative controls for each case. Test: 71 checks; 4 mutations detected. |
| B2 redaction incomplete | **Accepted.** Redacted tree-wide. *(This row first contained the search pattern itself, which re-published the names; that was corrected in L-021, see there.)* The names remain in git history, which is not rewritten on a public repository. L-018's M9 disposition was wrong; corrected here. |
| m1 thread tools | **Accepted**: documented as allowed for any thread ID, with low impact. |
| m2 test gaps | **Accepted**: `fork_repository` on `devos` tested; the test reads the command and matcher from `settings.json` and checks matcher coverage. |
| m3 lease hand-over | **Accepted**: the successor may take over a lease held by its own parent session (§2.2, `CLAUDE.md` step 3). |
| m4 `builder_check.sh` | **Accepted**: the holder is matched on the first backticked ID; `MODE run` or `MODE report` is printed; R1 requires `MODE run`. |
| m5 owned-ID workflow | **Accepted**: adding IDs returned by the builder's own create calls is a record change; other changes are high-impact. |
| m6 stale statements | **Accepted**: BP-03 cites T-A1a and T-A1b; T-H1 is retired; the deny-rule sentence in BP-06 is removed. |
| m7 review before merge is instruction only | **Accepted**: stated as such (G-015). |
| m8 T-H5 not traceable | **Accepted**: session and approximate time recorded; the tool error is the record. |
| m9 defence in depth | **Accepted**: noted in the register. |
| C4 live check in a builder-created session | **Accepted**: test T-H6 (pending at this entry). |

### L-022 · 2026-10-01 · T-H7 failed, then fixed: the recorder misread the observed response format

- When the builder created reviewer R-C00-BOM-5 (`session_01Y99Zfo6NNQUsTwGzyKckus`), the `PostToolUse` recorder did **not** append its ID: T-H7 failed.
- **Cause, found with a temporary debug hook** on a harmless self `get_session` call (removed afterwards): `PostToolUse` hooks do run for MCP tools, but `tool_response` arrives as a **list of `{"type":"text","text":"<JSON string>"}` items**. The recorder serialised that list, which escaped the quotes, so its pattern never matched. The unit test had used a different, assumed format. This is the FND-001 class again, now in a test fixture: an assumed format was taken as the real one.
- **Fix:** the recorder joins the text items before matching. The test now includes the observed format, captured from the debug dump.
- The reviewer's ID was added **by hand**, as a one-off exception. It is the builder's own create response, verifiable in this session's record. Hand edits are otherwise high-impact.
- The live re-test of T-H7 happens at the next `create_session`.
- Context note: this session is at about 77% context, beyond the 50% hand-over threshold of §2.1. It was not handed over, because PR #4, which installs the operating model, is not merged yet and needs this session's review loop. It hands over immediately after the merge (W-C00-05 remainder).

|---|---|

### L-027 · 2026-10-01 · T-B1 fails on condition 3; cause fixed; retest pre-registered

- **Result** (EV-C00-005). Conditions 1 and 2 are met: the probe booted through `CLAUDE.md`, found the live lease, did not take over and wrote nothing. Condition 3 is met only in part. It named the Next action row correctly, and noticed that the row was stale (PR #4 already merged). It did not say that heavy C00 items wait for the weekly reset. Under the reading fixed in L-026 before the result, that is a **FAIL**. The condition is not loosened.
- **Cause, two parts.**
  - The usage constraint was in the Stage row, not in the Next action row. A session that answers "what next" reports the Next action row.
  - The builder merged PR #4 without updating the Next action row in the same merge, so `main` carried a stale next action. This contradicts §3.2: `main` is the truth at every merge.
- **Fix.**
  - The Next action row is now self-contained: it carries the usage constraint and the reset time.
  - Rule, added to the practice of §3.2: a PR that completes the Next action also rewrites that row.
- **Positive observation.** The probe detected the stale row by checking `main`'s history against the state file. This is the drift check that §3.2 relies on.
- **T-B1r** is pre-registered in EV-C00-005 with the same three conditions, and runs after this entry is merged. Lease renewed to 23:05Z.

### L-029 · 2026-10-01 · T-A2 fails; dispatcher design changed (operating model v1.7)

**Result** (EV-C00-005). The one-shot routine fired at 20:08:36Z and delivered the message to the dispatcher `session_01FTnQrWv6hTTZyUV7zrJRBh`. **FAIL** on conditions 1 and 3:
- **Message unreadable.** Routine messages, and this session's own `send_later` messages, arrive as **queued notifications**, readable only with `ReadNotifications`. The builder's hook blocked that tool: the over-blocking that §9 had listed as "safe" since v1.6. The builder had seen the same notices in its own session and had not drawn the conclusion. That is a reasoning failure, recorded here.
- **Merge denied.** The session's automatic permission classifier denied the dispatcher's merge of its own record PR [#9](https://github.com/batuhanozgun/devos/pull/9) as a "merge without review", and later its read of `main` too.

Condition 2 was met: the decision was "start nothing", with the right reasons. The dispatcher's content (heartbeat 23:09 TR; decision; findings) is recorded here.

**PR #9 handling.** The builder **closed PR #9 without merging**. Merging it from this session would be a route around the classifier's denial. Its two findings are taken over below.

**Heartbeat.** The routine `trig_01D8eBZEmGsHvijrs4dQxNc8` is **disabled** until the fix is merged, so that it does not fire into a dispatcher that cannot act.

**Findings and changes** (v1.7; all high-impact; review R-C00-BOM-7 before merge):

| ID | Finding | Change |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | Routine and `send_later` messages are notifications; `ReadNotifications` was blocked | The hook allows `ReadNotifications`. It reads only this session's own queue, and its contents are untrusted data. The test moves it from a negative to a positive control. §9 updated; it is removed from the over-blocking list. |
| F2 | The classifier denies a dispatcher's self-merge | The dispatcher never merges. It keeps one standing PR from `claude/dispatcher`; each run reads and merges it at the boot check (§2.3, §3.2, R2). Stated cost: the heartbeat on `main` lags while no run is active. |
| F3 | Lease liveness used `get_session` "working"; an idle holder between turns would look dead (found by the dispatcher) | The lease is live until expiry, whatever the holder's status (§2.2, `CLAUDE.md` step 3, R2, failure table) |
| F4 | Whether the classifier lets the dispatcher start a run is unknown | Stated as an untested premise (§2.3, Appendix M); T-A2r tests it, with a denial treated as an S3 blocker |

**T-A2r** is pre-registered in EV-C00-005. Written at 2026-10-01T20:17Z.

### L-030 · 2026-10-01 · Review R-C00-BOM-7: PASS-WITH-CONDITIONS; conditions met

**Review.** `session_01F5o2EmcMv8CbNFLsvJdCq2`; verdict `evidence/C00/reviews/R-C00-BOM-7.md`, copied from `claude/review-R-C00-BOM-7` (`cdd8c3c`). **PASS-WITH-CONDITIONS** on `6d95709`. It found F1, F3 and F4 real and correctly fixed, the lease change strictly more conservative, and the T-A2r pre-registration timely. Conditions: B1, B2, m1.

**Correction to L-029 (B1).** The log is append-only, so the correction is stated here. L-029 says the classifier "denied the dispatcher's merge". The transcript shows a different sequence:
1. The merge call passed the classifier and failed at GitHub, because the SHA was short.
2. The classifier then blocked the dispatcher's next step toward the merge, `git rev-parse HEAD`, as "merge without review".
3. It also blocked a plain read of `main`.

The builder had summarised the dispatcher's own account without reading the transcript. That is the FND-001 class again: a claim was recorded at a strength its source did not support. **Rule:** a claim about another session's actions is checked against its transcript before it is recorded.

| Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|
| B1: "merge denied" misreported | **Accepted.** EV-C00-005 T-A2 row and §2.3 rewritten with the sequence; L-029 corrected here. |
| B2: runs merged the dispatcher PR with no scope check | **Accepted.** New `tools/check_dispatcher_pr.sh`: `DISPATCHER_PR OK` only if the PR changes nothing but the `DURUM.md` "Son nabız" line and appended stage-log lines; otherwise the run leaves the PR open and logs a finding. On PR #9's branch it gives OK. Three negative cases (hook edit, another `DURUM.md` line, an edited log line) give REFUSE (scratch worktree). §2.3, §3.2, R2 and Appendix M updated. Described as a scope check, not a review. |
| m1: the routine's stored prompt was still v1.6 | **Accepted.** The T-A2r setup now replaces it with the v1.7 R2 text. |
| m2: notification contents are a wider injection surface | **Accepted.** §9 names the sources, and every session acts only on a notification it expected. R2 tells the dispatcher to ignore other instructions. |
| m3: no explicit release at a clean stop | **Accepted.** §2.2 "Release": `Released <time>` replaces the expiry. `builder_check.sh` accepts a released lease. R2 and `CLAUDE.md` treat it as stale. The 3h15m bound was already enforced by `builder_check.sh`. |
| m4: premises and their strength | **Accepted.** The run's merge-ability is an untested premise. A denial is S3. A pass counts as "observed once". T-A2r condition 4 includes the scope check. (e), a negative-control notification, is not added: it costs another session, and R2 already says to ignore other text. |
| m5: the dispatcher's decision was right only through the usage clause under v1.6 | **Accepted**, in the EV row. |
| Classifier-denial rule: general, not only for `create_session` | **Accepted.** The §11 row now says: a denial is S3 for that action, never pursued through another tool, session or rewording. The old text "try a compliant route" is removed. |
| m6: no live evidence left for a non-MCP block | **Noted**: cite the next live block when one occurs. |
| m7: `DURUM.md` wording | **Accepted** in the next `DURUM.md` update. |

- **Lease** taken at 17:19Z in record PR #49 (state file only, L-032 lesson), expiry 20:19Z.
- **Finding F-036-1: Batu's answers were unrecorded for 35 hours.** Issue #6 has one comment by `batuhanozgun`, 2026-10-02T06:27Z, verbatim: "D-002: a, D-003: a". Interpretation: D-002 option (a), the stated default usage policy; D-003 option (a), accept the residual barrier risk until the audit environment (C02–C03), re-assessed at C03. Both equal the defaults already applied, so no work went wrong. The state file still said "no answer as of 2026-10-01T21:05Z", and `DURUM.md` still showed both as waiting. **Cause (D6):** boot step 6 is instructed; L-034 and L-035 were record entries by Batu's conversation session, which did not boot as a run and did not read the issue; no mechanism compares the issue with the state file. This is the "restated facts go stale" pattern (RUN_BRIEF §5 item 3) on a new surface, and an input to W-C00-12 (l). Recorded in PR #49; T-D1 receipt is now confirmed (Batu answered on the issue).
- **Classifier denial (recorded, §11).** Between 17:19Z and 17:23Z (clock read at 17:23Z) the builder tried to write `briefs/w-c00-12-counter-design/BATU_ORIGINALS_TR.md`, an extract of the "Original" sections of `briefs/w-c00-12/BATU_*_TR.md`, as input for the counter-design. The classifier denied it as "[Out-of-Place Publication]". The builder did not retry and does not pursue that outcome (no copy of Batu's texts in a new place). The counter-design brief instead names the existing files and tells the counter-designer to read only their "Original" sections. That republishes nothing. **Cost, stated:** the counter-designer opens files that also hold the builder's assessments, so its isolation from them rests on instruction only, like every reviewer's input restriction (§5). Its output must say whether it kept the restriction.
- **Counter-design brief** `briefs/w-c00-12-counter-design/BRIEF.md` (acceptance (d), RUN_BRIEF §2 step 3). It gives the goal, the constraints, Batu's recorded decisions, the plan and the library, and neutral summaries of platform facts and incidents. It withholds the current operating model, the state file, the log, the evidence, the run brief and every assessment. **Included on purpose (h):** the incident list, because the failures are observations of the environment the design must survive; it names no fix. **Left out on purpose:** the 24 OI-011 items, which mix observations with the builder's proposals.

### L-096 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: the guard decides every call with a written reason (D-008); built and unit-tested on `claude/w12-6-guard`; independent review requested

- **Discipline line (D1 to D9).** D1: the design rests on two documented, untested premises: a `PreToolUse` allow skips the prompt in Accept edits, and a `PermissionRequest` hook answers the prompts that remain (protected paths, critical paths); acceptance (i), the live check T-G3, tests both after the merge, and a failure reopens the item. D2: Batu's decision sets the mode, not the design. D3: the purpose is auditable decisions without a classifier. D4: the planted cases were written with the code, and the review is independent. ### L-098 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: re-review R-D008-2 FAIL on `61e03ae`; frame note on the program-identification layer; git moved to allow lists; a red-team pre-check; fixes on `claude/w12-6-guard`

- **Verdict.** R-D008-2 (reviewer `session_017kHX9NnejwdbkDB2hq8jL4`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-2` at `230bacb`, 18:13:22Z) is **FAIL**: the B-1 and B-2 reproductions were fixed, but the push ban (B3) and the live-tree ban (B4) were bypassed by a `git` alias (N-1), by push rerouting the guard could not see (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, a literal-URL push, a bare `--git-dir`; N-2), and B8 by several close variants of the dumps it named (N-3). - **Frame note (third round in this layer; operating model §9 history and §10).** R-C00-BOM-1 to 3 found a new route around the *session-tool* rules three times, and the answer then was a frame review, not a fourth patch. R-D008-1, R-D008-2 and the red-team below are the same shape in the *shell* layer: each found a new way to make the guard read a different command than bash runs. The response is not an endless deny list of command spellings. The frame: the guard decides by **(a)** identifying the program bash will run and **(b)** a rule keyed on that program. (a) is a bounded function — bash's command-word resolution: quote and continuation removal, globbing of the command word, alias expansion, wrapper option handling — and the findings were bugs in (a), now fixed by making (a) match bash or refuse when it cannot (an alias, an unknown subcommand, a non-literal command word, a `git` setting the guard cannot see are all refused). (b) for `git` moved from a deny list of risky settings to an **allow list** of subcommands, global options and settings, because git reads aliases, files and environment the guard cannot enumerate; anything not listed is denied. What stays residual, by the §9 threat model (accidents and injection, not a session that edits the guard): an interpreter (`python3 -c`), a sourced script, or a command the shell builds at run time. These are not plain commands and are stated in §9. A fourth finding of a *plain-command* bypass of (a) would reopen the frame, not add a patch.
- **Dispositions of R-D008-2.** N-1 (alias, unknown subcommand, `git-*` helper): fixed by the git allow list. N-2 (push reroute): fixed — the push address is resolved as git resolves it (`get-url --push --all`), a written address is denied, `--git-dir`/`--work-tree`/`--config-env` and `HOME`/`XDG_`/`GIT_` assignments are denied. N-3 (dump variants): fixed — combined flags are parsed (`-px` = `-p` + `-x`), `export`/`declare`/`compgen`/`ps e`/`find -exec env`/`/proc/*/env*` are covered, and `eval` and a run-time command name are G0. N-4 (`.git/` for file tools): fixed — the file tools now share `guarded_zone` with the shell. N-5 (log globs, tail truncation): fixed in part — globs and `find` under the log are denied, and the report prints the last record number so a later lower number shows an end-truncation; an interpreter rewriting the whole file stays residual. N-6 (Workflow run-time keys): the check denies the literal forms; a script that builds the option elsewhere is residual (stated, §9). N-7 (§9 lacked B11): the Shell row now states the git allow lists and B11. N-8 (effort on his own sessions): D-008 now states it.
- **Red-team pre-check** (a fresh-context subagent of this session, thinking independence only, not a binding review; on `0c7e55c`). It confirmed every prior route is closed and found three more bugs in program identification, all fixed here: a backslash-newline that split the program name (the guard joined with a space; bash joins with nothing), a glob in the command word (`/usr/bin/[g]it`; the command word must now be a plain literal), and a wrapper value-option eaten as the program (`exec -a foo git …`, `sudo -p x git …`; wrappers now consume their value options). It also found the file-tool `/etc/gitconfig` and `~/.config/git` gap (N-4, same fix) and that a NUL byte raised an internal error (now a clean G0). ### L-099 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: re-review R-D008-3 FAIL on `8672809`; the frame condition of L-098 was met; the shell analyzer now decomposes compound commands and denies what it cannot read

- **Verdict.** R-D008-3 (reviewer `session_013amfojmL8VjPWXKS7UBRYw`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-3` at `24753a7`, 18:46:18Z) is **FAIL**: every earlier reproduction stayed denied, but a bash reserved word in command position (`{ … }`, `if … then …`, a loop, `!`, `time`, `coproc`) hid the command that followed from every program-keyed rule (R3-1); a setting variable (`HOME`, `XDG_*`, `GIT_*`) set by a builtin without the text `NAME=` (`read`, `printf -v`, a `for` variable, `+=`) was not caught (R3-2); and a backtick substitution was not analysed (R3-3). All plain commands. Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-3.md`. Every check it ran passed on the head.
- **The frame condition of L-098 was met, as L-098 said it would be.** R3-1 is a plain-command bypass of step (a), program identification, so the response is not a fourth patch of the same shape but a change to how (a) works. Before: the analyzer took the first word of a simple command as the program, split only on control operators and grouping, and **allowed** anything whose program word matched no rule — so a reserved word in front, or a construct it did not model, let the real command through unchecked. Now: it splits on control operators, grouping **and every bash reserved word**, so the command inside a compound command is its own segment and is checked; it **denies what it cannot read** (a backtick, a run-time-built or glob command word, `eval`) instead of allowing it; and it checks the `for`/`select` loop variable and treats the loop's word-list as data. The reserved-word set is the full, fixed bash list, so no single construct hides a command. This is the structural flip the three rounds pointed to, not another spelling added to a deny list.
- **Honest boundary of the lexical approach.** The guard reads text; it does not run bash. What stays residual, by the §9 threat model (accidents and injection, not a session that edits the guard): an interpreter (`python3 -c`), a sourced file or a function body, and a command assembled at run time inside an interpreter. For data leaving the container the real backstop is the environment — the network policy (which hosts egress reaches) and the credential scope — not the guard; this is stated in §9 as the boundary, not claimed as a guard guarantee, and the network policy was not verified in this run.
- **Dispositions of R-D008-3.** R3-1: fixed by reserved-word decomposition; a planted case for each compound form (`{ }`, `if/then`, `for/do`, `until`, `!`, `coproc`, `select`, `function`, `time`, a pipeline into `{ }`), with the near misses `for f in *.md; do …`, `if [ -f x ]; then …` and `while read … done` staying allowed. R3-2: fixed — the loop variable, the `set`/`read`/`printf -v`/`export`/`declare`/`unset` name arguments and `NAME+=` are checked against the protected set (B11). R3-3: fixed — a backtick outside single quotes is G0; `$(…)` stays analysed. R3-4 (false positive): fixed — `[` and `[[` are allowed command words. R3-5 (bare-repo config): the push address is resolved as git resolves it, so the write is harmless on its own, and the R3-2 fix removes the path that used it; stated as residual. R3-6: the decision-log write now reports under its own wording, and the NUL note is narrowed to top-level string values.
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, 410 checks, including a check that no decision comes from an internal guard error and the R3-1 near misses; T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** Another adversarial pre-check aimed at bash grammar, then a narrow re-review R-D008-4 of the fixed head, created after its recorder line merges. The lease is renewed in that record PR.

### L-100 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: a second adversarial pass (bash grammar) on `67a53c9`; the shell analyzer now recurses into substitutions, for-lists and trap handlers; fixes pushed, R-D008-4 next

- **What the pass found** (a fresh-context subagent of this session, thinking independence only, not a binding review; on `67a53c9`). Four plain-command ways a command still reached execution unchecked: inside a double-quoted command substitution `"$(…)"` (the quotes stopped the tokenizer from splitting it; severest, and ordinary syntax); inside a `for`/`select` word-list substitution; inside a `trap` handler string; and a command line joined to a preceding comment by a backslash-newline. Also a file-tool gap: `~` was not expanded, so `~/.claude` and `~/.config/git` were not matched (the shell ban caught the same paths). It confirmed every earlier route stays closed and reported six false denials (a credential-looking substring inside a quoted argument or comment of an unrelated command).
- **This is the fifth round in the shell layer** (three reviews, two adversarial passes). The response is not another spelling: the analyzer now **finds and checks every context in which bash runs a command**, by a dedicated pass (`substitutions()`) that pulls out `$(…)`, `` `…` ``, `<(…)`/`>(…)` wherever they sit, including inside double quotes, and recurses the guard into each; the `for`/`select` list and the `trap` handler are recursed the same way; the comment-and-continuation handling was corrected; and `$((…))` arithmetic and `$'…'` literals are read as non-commands. What it cannot read it still denies. The false denials were from scanning the raw command text for credential and setting-variable patterns; those scans now run on the individual tokens, so a credential name quoted inside an unrelated command's argument, or in a comment, no longer trips them.
- **Stopping rule set for the next round.** The remaining residual is a true interpreter, a sourced file, or a command a program assembles at run time — not plain shell, and past this guard's threat model (§9). If the binding re-review R-D008-4 still finds a *plain-command* shell bypass, the builder stops patching the parser and brings Batu the design trade-off (a narrower shell posture, or accepting the environment as the primary boundary with the guard as best-effort), rather than a sixth patch.
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, 438 checks, including the four new bypass forms denied and the six false-denial near misses allowed; T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** The binding re-review R-D008-4 of the fixed head, created after its recorder line merges.

### L-102 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-5 FAIL (one plain-command write bypass, B-1); B5 inverted to a path-based ban; L-100 routing corrected; R-D008-6 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-5 (reviewer `session_01G8JqdayC2Kt6FxTpSYGCHr`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-5` at `7a65dd5`, 19:35:15Z) is **FAIL**: one plain-command bypass of B5 — a write into a guarded location through a redirection the guard did not count (`>&`, `<>`) or through a writer program not on its list (for example `sort -o`, `patch`, archive extraction). The mechanical checks passed and no other ban broke. Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-5.md`.
- **Routing correction (FR-02).** L-100's stopping rule said this finding would mean "bring Batu the design trade-off". That wording mis-routed a technical matter to the customer: FR-02 §6 "Never his" bullet 3 is binding — a rule the builder introduced (the guard) is changed by the builder through its own independent review (§5, PC-05), never put to Batu, who has twice declined technical and permission decisions (D-006, D-007). The stopping rule's real content holds: stop blind patching and make the change structural. So this is not a sixth enumeration patch and not a question for Batu; it is the structural completion, judged by R-D008-6. Batu was told the state, not asked to decide.
- **The fix (B-1).** B5 is inverted the way the git layer was in L-098: it no longer enumerates writing programs. It keys on the guarded locations, a small fixed set. A write is caught either as a redirection output target — now including `>&` and `<>` when the target is a file, not a descriptor — or because a program that is not a pure reader names a guarded location as an argument or option value. Pure readers (`cat`, `grep`, `sed -n`, `sort` without `-o`, `wc`, `diff`, `awk` reading, …) may name a guarded path; everything else that does is denied. This holds against `sort -o`, `patch`, `tar -C`, `ex`, `install` and any other writer without listing them.
- **This is the last enumeration-based ban.** Across five rounds each layer that was a deny list became structural: remotes and branches (allow list), git subcommands and settings (allow list, L-098), the places a command can run (recurse into substitutions, for-lists and traps, L-099/L-100), and now writes to guarded locations (path-based). What remains residual is stated honestly in §9 and is past this guard's threat model: a true interpreter running its own language (`python3 -c`, an `awk` program's own redirection), a sourced file, a run-time-assembled command; and, for data leaving the container, the environment's network policy and credential scope, not the guard. The worst local outcome, a session swapping its own guard (L-088), is now blocked by the path ban regardless of the write method.
- **Dispositions.** B-1: fixed structurally (above). m-1 (PermissionRequest has no `|| exit 2` wrapper, so a missing interpreter there leaves the prompt open): in practice the PreToolUse wrapper fires first and denies a call a missing interpreter would hit, so acceptance (g) is met at PreToolUse; the PermissionRequest text in §9 already states the open-prompt case, and no wrapper is added because exit 2 is not honored on PermissionRequest (documented). Noted, not changed.
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, 459 checks, with the B-1 write routes denied and descriptor redirections and reads of guarded files allowed; T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.

### L-103 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-6 FAIL; the lexical guard is accepted as best-effort for live-guard writes (D-003, D-008 cost 1); merge-gate code guarded; copy hardening; R-D008-7 next

- **Why this is accepted, not escalated to Batu.** §9's threat model has always said a repo-editable hook "cannot be a barrier against a builder that intends to bypass it; what it can do is stop accidents and injected instructions." D-003 (Batu, 2026-10-02) accepted the editable-hook residual until the audit environment. D-008 cost 1 (stated to Batu and accepted) was that removing the classifier leaves only the written rules. B-1 is exactly that residual: a data-derived write the written rules cannot name. It is within two of Batu's own accepted decisions, so by FR-02 it is not a new question for him; the acceptance of best-effort is a reviewer/closure judgement on the builder's own rule.
- **Dispositions.** B-1: accepted as a stated residual (§9 "Edits of `.claude/**`", "Best-effort"); cheap hardening added — a copy-like command (`cp`, `tar`, `rsync`, `cpio`, `unzip`, `install`, …) that names a `.claude` or `.git` path component is denied (the honest-mistake `cp -r …/.claude <root>` case), which the test covers; the general archive/patch route stays residual, named in §9. B-2: fixed — the merge gate's code (`tools/check_records.py`, `tools/records.py`) is now write-guarded in the live tree (denied to the file tools, redirections, `sed -i` and writer programs) while still runnable, so M7 no longer depends on a file any tool could silently change. m-1: `test`, `[`, `[[`, `jq`, `yq` added to the readers (removing false denials on guarded-file reads); the duplicate reader entries removed.
- **This closes the lexical-hardening program.** Every ban is now structural (allow lists for remotes/branches/git, recursion into all command contexts, a path-based write ban) with an honest, bounded residual: interpreters and a program's own scripting, sourced files, run-time assembly, and data-derived writes into the live guard — all past a repo-editable hook's reach, with the environment and git history as the backstop (D-003). A drift check is recorded as a stronger C02–C03 option.
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, 459 checks (the data-derived copy denied, the gate code runnable but not writable, the reader false-denials gone); T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-7 judges the guard against the §9 threat model with the now-honest residual — whether the design is sound for accidents and injection and the residual is stated fairly — rather than whether an adversary can find any bypass (which R-D008-6 established is not achievable lexically and is not the acceptance bar). If R-D008-7 disputes the accepted best-effort posture itself, that is a disagreement about D-003/D-008, which a second reviewer or Batu settles (§5), not a seventh guard patch.

### L-105 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-8 (narrow FAIL); tools/ made a guarded location and git output-file options caught (B8-1, B8-2); R-D008-9 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-8 (reviewer `session_0113cXjsQ3Ha4SMmFFs6knBx`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-8` at `4e20ad8`, 20:18:03Z) is a **narrow FAIL**: B7-2 is fixed and the record's count is honest (481 on the pushed branch), and B7-1's listed cases all pass, but two named-target write routes remained — not the accepted data-derived residual, so blocking. - **B8-1 (blocking): `tools/` was guarded only by the writer lists, not the catch-all.** A write by a program outside the known writer lists (`patch`, `split`, `ed`, `shred`, `unlink`, an archive extracted with `-C tools`, a download-to-file option) reached the gate code, and a glob on the directory component (`too*/r*.py`) slipped the exact-path test. **Fixed:** the live `tools/` directory is now a guarded location in `guarded_zone` itself, so it gets the same glob, ancestor and new-file handling as `.claude/`; `write_guarded` is now just `guarded_zone`. The catch-all ("a non-reader naming a guarded path is a write") now runs against `tools/` too, and it exempts readers and interpreters, so `python3 tools/check_records.py`, `bash tools/test_*.sh`, `cat`/`grep` on `tools/` stay allowed while `patch`, `split`, a download tool and the directory glob are denied. A write a true interpreter performs through its own language stays the stated residual.
- **B8-2 (blocking, pre-existing): git's own output-file options wrote a named file anywhere.** `git show|log|diff --output=<guarded>` and `git archive|format-patch|bundle -o <guarded>` wrote committed content into the live `.claude/`, `.git/hooks/` or `tools/`, unseen by B4. **Fixed:** `git_checks` now treats `--output[=]` and the `-o`/`--output-directory` of `archive`/`format-patch`/`bundle` as write destinations, checked with `write_guarded`; the same option to a path outside the zones (`/tmp/...`) stays allowed.
- **Honesty.** §9's `tools/` sentence is rewritten to say the directory is a guarded location written only through the checked routes, naming B8-1/B8-2; L-104's count clause is clarified here as "481 on the pushed branch" (a detached checkout prints 478, with three branch controls skipped as the script states).

### L-106 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-9 (narrow FAIL, one a regression I introduced); interpreter exemption narrowed, git positional dests and format-patch spellings caught; R-D008-10 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-9 (reviewer `session_018NPMKTMSjWjgCNLVprCFLv`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-9` at `ea55e0b`, 20:30:01Z) is a **narrow FAIL** with three items, all named-target routes (not the accepted residual). Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-9.md`. B8-1 and B8-2's listed cases all pass; the suites pass (502).
- **B9-1 (blocking, a regression this work introduced).** The interpreter exemption added for B8-1 was applied to every guarded zone, not only `tools/`. So an interpreter naming a live `.claude/` or `.git/` path as an *output* (for example a standard-library module CLI writing its second argument into `.claude/settings.json`) moved from denied (at the prior head) to allowed — the guard was made weaker for `.claude/` than before. **Fixed:** the exemption now covers only an interpreter's *script* argument (the first non-option arg, and not after `-m`/`-c`) when that script is under `tools/`; every other argument it names, and any script it names under `.claude/`/`.git/`, goes through the catch-all. `python3 tools/check_records.py` stays allowed; `python3 -m json.tool x .claude/settings.json` and `python3 .claude/hooks/…` are denied, as before the regression.
- **B9-2 (blocking, pre-existing): git subcommands that write a named tree.** `git clone`, `git worktree add` and `git init` (and `init --separate-git-dir=`) with a destination in `.claude/`, `.git/` or `tools/` were allowed; a clone into `tools/` re-opens the import-shadow route, a clone/worktree into `.claude/agents` or `skills` adds session configuration. **Fixed:** `git_checks` now treats each such positional destination as a write, checked with `write_guarded`; the same into `/tmp` stays allowed. (`submodule` is not on the git subcommand allow list, so it is already denied.)
- **B9-3 (blocking as record accuracy): the B8-2 parser missed `--output-directory=` and a bundled short `-…o`.** **Fixed:** both forms are now matched. These write a directory of patch files with git-chosen names, so they could not replace a guard file; the record is corrected to match the code.
- **Honesty.** §9's `tools/` sentence is rewritten to state the interpreter-is-a-reader-only-for-a-tools-script rule and the git positional-tree rule, and to name B9-1/B9-2/B9-3. The 502 count was accurate; it is 521 after the new cases.
- **The named-target-write class is now swept.** A guarded path named on a command line is a write unless it is a reader's read or an interpreter's `tools/` script: covered by the catch-all over all zones; redirections, writer lists, git output options and git named-tree destinations all route to `write_guarded`. What remains is the accepted residual: a path that comes from data (an archive, a patch, a copied tree) or from inside an interpreter's own program. This is the fourth consecutive round whose findings were implementation gaps in that one class, now closed at the class level rather than case by case.
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, 521 checks; T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-10, scoped to B9-1/B9-2/B9-3 and the §9 wording. If it finds only a fresh instance of the accepted data-derived/interpreter residual, that is a PASS under the agreed terms, not a tenth fix.
- **Record changes:** evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-9.md · addition · the R-D008-9 verdict, copied verbatim from its review branch

### L-107 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-10 (narrow FAIL, three items, one a regression I introduced); git tree-writes and the interpreter exemption moved from per-slot parsing to coarse structural rules; R-D008-11 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-10 (reviewer `session_01Tm4QVMVK9AAL3v5A6F5RZZ`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-10` at `3d0aacb`, 20:43:54Z) is a **narrow FAIL** with three items on PR #117 head `53b89d3`. Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-10.md`. B9-1's regression is confirmed fixed, the listed B9-2/B9-3 cases pass, and both suites pass (521).
- **Decision: stop fine-grained parsing of git destinations and interpreter options.** Two of the last three findings (B9-1 at R-D008-9, B10-1 here) were regressions this work introduced while parsing per-slot git destinations and attached short options. Precise parsing of a space the guard does not control (git's and each interpreter's own option grammar) has negative returns. This round replaces it with coarse structural rules, the same move that earlier ended the B5/compound/substitution rounds.
- **B10-1 (blocking, a regression `53b89d3` introduced).** The attached `-o<path>` parse used a greedy `re.sub(r"^-[A-Za-z]*o", "", a2)`; for `-otools/x.tar` it stripped to the last letter `o`, yielding `ls/x.tar` (not guarded), so `git archive -otools/x.tar` was allowed. **Fixed:** the attached value is now matched non-greedily from the first `o` (`-[A-Za-z]*?o(.+)$`), and only for `archive`/`format-patch`/`bundle`, where `-o` is an output path (for `clone` `-o` is a remote name, for `worktree` `-b` a branch — not paths).
- **B10-2 (blocking, B9-2 incomplete): named-tree destinations outside the expected positional slot.** The per-slot `dests` logic missed the implicit destination of `git -C <guarded> clone/init` (no destination argument; git uses the `-C` directory), the `worktree add -b <branch> <dest>` slot (the branch name took the destination's place), and `worktree move`. **Fixed coarsely:** for the tree-writing subcommands (`clone`, `init`, `worktree`, `archive`, `format-patch`, and the already-denied `submodule`/`bundle`/`checkout-index`) the guard now denies when the effective `-C`/cwd directory is itself a guarded zone, or cannot be resolved, or when **any** positional argument or `--opt=value` resolves into a guarded zone — instead of enumerating each subcommand's destination slots. There is no reason to create a tree inside `.claude/`, `tools/` or `.git/`, so the over-denial this adds (a branch or remote named exactly like a guarded path, or a revision/pathspec that is one, is refused) is a visible, explained denial, not a hole. `clone`/`worktree add`/`init`/`archive` into `/tmp` or a scratch directory stay allowed.
- **B10-3 (blocking, small): attached `-m<mod>` kept the interpreter exemption.** Only the exact words `-m`/`-c`/`-e` ended the "running a `tools/` script" scan, so `python3 -mzipfile tools/<f>` exempted the module's first argument. **Fixed:** the exemption now holds only when **every** option before the script is on a small no-value allow list (`SAFE_INTERP_OPTS`: `-B -u -O -S -I -E -s` and similar); any other option — `-m`, `-c`, `-e`, `-W`, a value-taker, an attached `-m<mod>`/`-c<code>`, or a bare `-` — ends the exemption, so the script and every other argument are then checked as ordinary paths. Being generous in that allow list usually only over-denies (the script, under `tools/`, is then checked and denied); but one list shared across interpreters with different grammars is **not** safe in general — R-D008-11 B11-3 (L-108) showed a shell `-s` makes standard input the program, so the list is now read per interpreter.
- **Honesty (R-D008-10 condition 4).** §9's sentence no longer says a git subcommand "that writes a named tree" is denied by slot; it now states the tree-writers are checked coarsely (the directory they run in and every path they name) and that the interpreter exemption requires a `tools/` script preceded only by no-value options, citing R-D008-10 B10-1/B10-2/B10-3. I am **not** claiming the class is closed beyond what the code checks: a guarded path that appears **literally** as a command-line token (a positional, an `--opt=value`, an `-o<path>`, a tree-writer's effective directory, or an interpreter argument when the exemption does not apply) is denied. (R-D008-11 corrected this where the token is not literal: a guarded path reached behind an unevaluated shell variable or command substitution is denied only when a guarded component — `.claude`, `.git`, `tools`, `.config/git`, a protected name — is still literally visible in the token (B11-1/B11-2, L-108); a bare variable that expands to a guarded path with nothing guarded visible is part of the residual.) A write whose path comes from inside an interpreter's own program (`python3 -c`, `perl -e`, an `awk` redirection) or from data (an archive, a patch, a copied tree) remains the accepted residual (§9; D-003; D-008 cost 1), which the reviewer re-confirmed as not blocking.
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, **539** checks, 0 BAD (new planted cases: attached `-o` into `tools/`; `-C <guarded>` clone/init; `worktree add -b` slot and `worktree move`; attached `-mzipfile`; separate `-c`; bare `-`; and the allowed near misses — attached `-o` to `/tmp`, `clone -o upstream`, `worktree add -b` to `/tmp`, `-mjson.tool` to `/tmp`, safe-option-prefixed `tools/` scripts); T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-11, scoped to B10-1/B10-2/B10-3 and this §9/L-107 wording, on the fixed head. If it passes (or passes with conditions I meet), the merge gate's verdict requirement is satisfied and PR #117 can merge; the own-language/data-derived residual is accepted and not re-opened.
- **Record changes:** evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-10.md · addition · the R-D008-10 verdict, copied verbatim from its review branch

### L-108 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-11 (FAIL, three items); substitution placeholder made unresolvable, variable/substitution write targets caught, shell -s dropped; honesty corrections; R-D008-12 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-11 (reviewer `session_01GAAaKvSheFMKPb72BCEoFq`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-11` at `559a094`, 2026-10-04) is a **FAIL** with three items on PR #117 head `d3f0f09`. Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-11.md`. B10-1/B10-2/B10-3 are confirmed fixed, the listed cases behave as required, the suites pass (539), and the daily commands work. The three new items are all command-line paths the lexical pass did not fully resolve (not the data-derived residual).
- **B11-1 (blocking, criterion 2): a command substitution became a plain placeholder word, so an unresolvable directory looked resolved.** `substitutions()` replaced `$(…)` / `` `…` `` with `_sub_`, which has no `$`, so `resolve_dir`/`target_path` treated `-C $(pwd)/tools` as the concrete path `<root>/_sub_/tools` (not guarded) and allowed `git -C $(pwd)/tools init`. **Fixed:** the placeholder is now `$_sub_`; it carries a `$`, so `resolve_dir`/`target_path` return unresolvable, the tree-writers' `e is None` denial fires, and a substitution used as a command name now hits G0 (consistent with §9). Over-denial recorded: `git -C $(mktemp -d) init` is now denied (planted).
- **B11-2 (blocking, criterion 4 honesty; pre-existing): a write target that starts with a variable passed every write check.** `target_path` returns `None` for a `$`, and `None` was read as "not guarded", so `echo x > $PWD/.claude/settings.json`, `cp /tmp/a $PWD/tools/check_records.py`, `git clone … $PWD/tools/x` and `git archive -o $PWD/tools/x.tar` were allowed. **Fixed:** a new `guarded_write(d, arg)` wraps every write site (redirections, the writer loop, the catch-all, git `out_guarded`); when the path is unresolvable it falls back to `literal_guarded(arg)`, which denies the write if a guarded component (`.claude`, `.git`, `tools`, `.config/git`, a protected name) is still literally visible after dropping the `$`-bearing parts. A bare variable (`$DEST`) with nothing guarded visible stays the lexical residual, now stated honestly in §9 and in the corrected L-107.
- **B11-3 (blocking, small, criterion 3 intent): one `SAFE_INTERP_OPTS` list across all interpreters let `-s` keep the exemption for shells, where `-s` makes standard input the program.** `bash -s tools/<f>` exempted `tools/<f>`, which is really an argument to a stdin program that can write it. **Fixed:** the list is now read per interpreter — for the shell family (`bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `dash`, `ksh`) `-s` and `-i` are dropped, so `bash -s tools/x` is denied while `bash -x tools/x.sh` and `python3 -s tools/x.py` stay allowed. The "can never open a hole" wording in the code comment and L-107 is corrected.
- **Honesty.** The false claims the reviewer named are corrected: §9 no longer says the guard "blocks every write that names a guarded location" without qualification (it now states the variable/substitution rule and the residual), the L-107 "any command-line token is denied" clause is narrowed to literal tokens with the B11-1/B11-2 correction, and the L-107 / code-comment "can never open a hole" line is corrected (B11-3). The 539 count was taken on a pushed-branch checkout (a detached checkout prints 536 with branch controls skipped, R-D008-11 m-3).
- **Tests on the fixed revision** (scratch clone): T-G1 `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh` ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, **560** checks, 0 BAD (new planted cases: `$(pwd)`/backtick/`cd`-substitution tree-writes and `$(pwd)/.claude` redirection; `$PWD/.claude`, `$PWD/tools`, `cp`/`clone`/`archive` into `$PWD/tools`, `$HOME/.config/git`; `bash/sh/ksh -s tools/…`; and the allowed near misses — non-guarded `$TMPDIR`/`$HOME`/`$DEST` targets, a substitution `cd` to a non-guarded dir, `bash -x tools/…`, `python3 -E -s -S -I tools/…`); T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9 of 9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-12, scoped to B11-1/B11-2/B11-3 and this §9/L-107/L-108 wording, on the fixed head. The data-derived and bare-variable/own-language residuals are accepted (D-003, D-008 cost 1) and not re-opened; if the review finds only a fresh instance of those, that is a PASS under the agreed terms.

### L-109 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-12 (FAIL, a six-item sweep); the cwd/tilde/brace/reader-writer holes fixed and the lexical guard restated honestly as best-effort; R-D008-13 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-12 (reviewer `session_01EZ4A3xdrfFySDGQum6QxUh`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-12` at `559a094`... see file; 2026-10-04) is a **FAIL** on PR #117 head `854cb8c`. Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-12.md`. B11-1/B11-2/B11-3 are confirmed fixed and the suites pass; the adversarial sweep (which this round asked for, to flush the class in one pass) found six more command-line spellings that reach a guarded write, four needing no variable. All are honest-mistake-shaped, so fixed rather than deferred; the fixes are cheap and local.
- **B12-1 (cwd set by an unresolvable `cd`/`pushd` with a visible guarded component).** `cd $PWD/tools && echo x > check_records.py` left `d=None`, and a relative write after it was unchecked. **Fixed:** such a `cd`/`pushd` is denied (the guard cannot track writes in a guarded directory named by a variable/substitution). `cd $DEST` / `cd $(mktemp -d)` with nothing guarded visible stay allowed (residual).
- **B12-2 (`cd`/`pushd` options read as the directory).** `cd -P tools && touch x` resolved the cwd to `<root>/-P`. **Fixed:** the operand finder skips `-L -P -e -@ -- -n`, so the real directory is resolved (and `cd -P tools` is then caught); `-` and `+N`/`-N` are treated as unknown.
- **B12-3 (`env -C DIR` / `--chdir`).** `env -C tools touch x` changed the directory invisibly (the value was skipped by `strip_wrappers`). **Fixed:** `env -C`/`--chdir` (separate, attached or `=`) is denied; it has no daily use here.
- **B12-4 (tilde forms other than `~`/`~/`).** `echo x > ~+/tools/x` resolved to `<root>/~+/tools/x`. **Fixed:** `target_path` returns unresolvable for `~+`, `~-`, `~user` (matching `resolve_dir`), so `literal_guarded` catches the visible `tools`/`.claude`.
- **B12-5 (brace expansion).** `touch {tools,tmp}/x` hid the component. **Fixed:** `target_path` treats a `{`/`}` token as unresolvable and `literal_guarded` splits on `/ { } ,`, so the `tools` component is seen. ANSI-C `$'tools'` quoting is left as a stated residual (an own-quoting form, adjacent to the interpreter-own-language residual).
- **B12-6 (writers the guard treated as readers / unknown in-place flags).** `xxd -r … tools/x`, `yq -i … .claude/settings.json`, `gawk -i inplace … tools/<f>`. **Fixed:** `xxd`, `yq` and the `awk` family are removed from the blanket readers and made readers only without their write flag (`xxd -r`, `yq -i`, `awk`/`gawk` with `inplace`), so with the flag their operands are checked as writes; `jq` (no in-place mode) stays a pure reader.
- **m-1 / m-2.** `literal_guarded` now also matches the decision-log dir name (`devos-guard`) and `/etc/gitconfig`, and, for a removal, a visible `.config` ancestor of `~/.config/git` (`rm -rf $HOME/.config`).
- **The honest restatement (B12-7).** §9 no longer says the guard "blocks every write that names a guarded location". It now says the guard catches guarded writes in their **plain and common forms** (redirection, listed writers, git tree-writers and output options, interpreter arguments, a component visible literally or behind a variable/substitution/brace/tilde, and the cwd set by `cd`/`pushd` with `env -C` denied), and that the rest is **best-effort, not complete**: a bare variable with nothing guarded visible, a component hidden in `$'…'` or other quoting, an unlisted writer/in-place flag, a path produced in an interpreter's own language, or a data-derived write. For the whole tail the barrier is the merge gate's independent review, the git history and the environment (D-003, D-008 cost 1) — not the guard. This is why the chase ends here: rounds R-D008-6..12 established the class is lexically unbounded, and the posture Batu already accepted covers it.
- **Tests** (scratch clone): T-G1 ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, **581** checks, 0 BAD (new planted cases for B12-1..6, m-1, m-2, and the allowed near misses — `cd` options/targets to a scratch dir, a bare-variable cwd, `cd $(mktemp -d)`, and plain `xxd`/`yq`/`gawk`/`jq` reads); T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9/9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-13, on the fixed head, framed so a newly-found EXOTIC spelling (one not in the plain forms the suite plants) is judged against the honest best-effort statement above and the accepted residual (D-003, D-008 cost 1): noted, but not blocking, because the barrier for it is review-before-merge. A genuinely plain/common honest-mistake spelling that is still open would block.
