### L-110 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-13 (narrow FAIL, two plain holes) under the best-effort standard; fd-numbered redirection and bundled sed/perl -i fixed; R-D008-14 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-13 (reviewer `session_01Emq1DuC2zs6pFCdzDnx8ZE`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-13`; 2026-10-04) is a **narrow FAIL** on PR #117 head `6ddf311`, judged under the stated best-effort standard (only a PLAIN/COMMON honest-mistake spelling blocks; an exotic/determined spelling is accepted residual). Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-13.md`. It confirms all six R-D008-12 fixes and both minors, both suites pass, and §9's best-effort posture is honest. The standard held: the sweep's exotic cases (`$'tools'`, a bare variable, `python3 -c`, `perl -e <script>`, `tar -xf` in the root) were classified as accepted residual, not findings. Two plain holes block; both one-line.
- **B13-1 (blocking, plain): a leading IO number hid a `cp`/`install`/`ln` destination.** The tokenizer emits the `2` of `2>…`/`1>…`/`2>&1` as an ordinary word, so `written = plain[-1:]` took the fd number as the destination and `cp /tmp/a tools/check_records.py 2>/dev/null` (and `2>&1`, `1>/dev/null`, into `tools/`, `~/.config/git`, `/etc/gitconfig`, the log dir) was allowed. Silencing stderr after a `cp` is one of the most common things a builder or an injected "copy into tools/" instruction writes. **Fixed:** in the redirection split, an all-digit word immediately before a redirection operator is popped as the IO number, not kept as an argument; a non-guarded destination and a `tools/` source stay allowed.
- **B13-2 (blocking, plain): `-i` bundled with other short options was missed for `sed`/`perl`.** Only an argument starting with `-i` counted, so `perl -pi -e` (the canonical idiom), `perl -0pi`, `perl -lpi`, `sed -ri`, `sed -Ei`, `sed -ni` edited `.claude/`/`tools/` in place unchecked. **Fixed:** a new `inplace_flag(args)` treats `--in-place` and any single-dash short cluster containing `i` as in-place, used by both the in-place write check and the `sed`-reader exception so they agree; read-only `sed -E`/`perl -ne` stay allowed.
- **Honesty (n-1).** §9's opening design-goal bullet no longer claims "every route an honest builder might take by mistake is blocked technically"; it now says the plain routes are blocked and points to the best-effort/residual statement. The B13-1/B13-2 overclaim the reviewer noted is resolved by the fixes themselves (those plain forms with listed writers are now caught).
- **Tests** (scratch clone): T-G1 ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, **599** checks, 0 BAD (new planted cases for B13-1 — fd-numbered redirections after `cp`/`install`/`ln` into `tools/` and `~/.config/git`, with non-guarded-dest and `tools/`-source allowed — and B13-2 — `perl -pi/-0pi`, `sed -ri/-Ei/-ni` denied, `sed -E`/`perl -ne` reads allowed, `echo hi >&2` allowed); T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9/9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-14, on the fixed head, under the same best-effort standard. The reviewer stated it expects a pass once B13-1/B13-2 are fixed with tests; the accepted residual is not re-opened.
- **Record changes:** evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-13.md · addition · the R-D008-13 verdict, copied verbatim from its review branch

### L-111 · 2026-10-04 · W-C00-12.6: review R-D008-14 (narrow FAIL, two more bundled-cluster holes) under the best-effort standard; one shared helper closes the class for listed value-options; R-D008-15 next

- **Verdict.** R-D008-14 (reviewer `session_01JkwBCFp6tEpQhR2goFscZy`; branch `claude/review-R-D008-14`; 2026-10-04) is a **narrow FAIL** on PR #117 head `bca1ce5`, judged under the best-effort standard. Copied verbatim to `evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-14.md`. It confirms B13-1/B13-2 fixed, no regression, both suites pass (599), and §9 honest; the earlier B11/B12 fixes all still hold. The sweep's exotic cases (`sed --in`, `yq -Pi` on a less-common tool, `xxd` two-positional, `cd -` state, interpreter own-language, data-derived) were classified accepted residual. Two plain holes block, both the same class as B13-2 (a value-taking short option bundled in a cluster).
- **B14-1 (blocking, plain): a bundled/attached `-t` on `cp`/`install`/`ln` hid the destination.** `cp -rt tools /tmp/a`, `cp -vt tools …`, `cp -ttools …`, `install -Dt tools …`, `ln -st tools …`, `cp -rt tools/ /tmp/check_records.py` (overwrites the gate checker), and the directory forms `cp -vt /etc /tmp/gitconfig` and into the log dir were allowed, because `tdir` matched only the exact words `-t`/`--target-directory[=]`. `cp -rt DIR`/`install -Dt DIR` are everyday GNU idioms. **Fixed:** a shared `cluster_opt_value(args, letter)` returns the value of a value-option bundled in a single-dash cluster (the cluster text after the first `letter`, else the next word); the `-t` value is unioned with `plain[-1:]` (never replaces it, so a mis-read value only over-denies), and, because a copy INTO a directory writes `dir/basename(source)`, each `dir/basename(src)` is also checked — so `cp -vt /etc /tmp/gitconfig` (→ `/etc/gitconfig`) is caught too. The same into `/tmp` stays allowed. (This round's union also kept `plain[-1:]`, which for a `-t` copy is a SOURCE, so a `tools/` source copied OUT with `-t` — `cp -t /tmp/d tools/x` — was wrongly denied; that is the C15-1 regression, fixed in L-112 by parsing the `-t` directory with `target_dirs` instead of unioning `plain[-1:]`.)
- **B14-2 (blocking, plain, lower severity): `sort -o` bundled in a cluster was missed.** `sort -uo tools/x tools/x` (the in-place de-dup idiom), `sort -no`, `sort -ro` into `tools/`/`.claude/`/`~/.config/git` were allowed, because the `sort` reader exception matched only `-o`/`--output`. **Fixed:** `sort` is a writer when `cluster_opt_value(args, "o")` is non-empty, so a bundled `-o` moves it out of the readers and its operands are checked.
- **Also closed (was accepted residual): `yq` bundled `-i`.** `yq -Pi … tools/x.yml` is now denied (the `yq` reader exception treats any single-dash cluster containing `i`, or `--inplace`, as in place), at no cost via the same cluster idea.
- **Still accepted residual (stated, not fixed):** `xxd`'s two-positional output mode (`xxd in out`), `sed --in` long-option abbreviation, `cd -`/stateful-cwd tricks, a digit-named destination before a spaced redirection, and the interpreter-own-language and data-derived writes. These are exotic under the §9 best-effort posture; the barrier is the merge gate's review.
- **Honesty.** The R-D008-13 n-1 fix stands. The §9 "plain and common forms / listed writers" statement is now true of `cp/install/ln -t`, `sort -o` and `yq -i` in their bundled forms too.
- **Tests** (scratch clone): T-G1 ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS, **613** checks, 0 BAD (new planted cases for B14-1 — bundled/attached `-t` on `cp`/`install`/`ln` into `tools/`, `/etc/gitconfig`, and the gate checker, with non-guarded `-t` dirs allowed (a `tools/`-source-copied-out allow case is added in L-112, R-D008-15 C15-1) — B14-2 — `sort -uo`/`-no` denied, `sort -u` read allowed — and `yq -Pi` denied); T-G2 `tools/test_merge_gate.py` 9/9; `check_records.py all` RECORDS PASS; `records.py render --check` OK.
- **Next.** R-D008-15, on the fixed head, under the same best-effort standard. The reviewer stated it expects a pass once B14-1/B14-2 are fixed with tests; the shared `cluster_opt_value` helper closes the bundled-cluster class for the value-options the guard lists.
- **Record changes:** evidence/C00/reviews/R-D008-14.md · addition · the R-D008-14 verdict, copied verbatim from its review branch

### L-119 · 2026-10-05 · W-C00-12: CR-1 observed — a session in Accept edits cannot start sessions; D-009 for Batu; the run continues with session-free work

- **Observed (twice).** This run's first `create_session`, the W-C00-12.6 acceptance verifier (review ID `R-W126-ACC-1`, brief `Task-Brief: W-C00-12.6 verifier 0011c3867459ebe6` generated for target `ed2619b`), was refused by the platform: "MCP tool call requires approval". The guard had allowed it on PreToolUse (decision #81, 09:39:45Z, rule S2) and answered the PermissionRequest with allow (#82). The call named the builder environment, model `claude-opus-5-5` and `permission_mode` `acceptEdits`, the caller's own mode. One diagnostic call followed, to tell two readings apart (the environment, or the caller's mode): the same call without `environment_id` (#86 and #87 at 09:41:53Z, both allow) got the same refusal. No further attempt was made.
- **Cause (documented).** `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions`, fetched at 09:45:43Z (guard record #137): "MCP tools marked `requiresUserInteraction` also still prompt when a hook returns `"allow"`, as do connector tools your organization set to `ask`"; the MCP page adds that an organization's `ask` prompts even in `acceptEdits`, `auto` and `bypassPermissions`. In a session nobody attends, the prompt is refused. The platform's own page on session permissions says the mode is set by the surface that started the session and cannot be changed from inside it. So no hook, allow rule or repository setting lets a session in Accept edits start sessions. Which of the two mechanisms applies to the session tools is not documented. A researcher subagent (fresh context, `claude-code-guide`) gathered the pages; the quoted sentences were then read by this session on the fetched page itself.
- **CR-1 corrected.** The parent's working reading ("a session cannot create a child in a more permissive mode than its own") is refuted: the child here asked for the caller's own mode. Observed: a session in `acceptEdits` cannot start any session; the parent's account adds the same for `default` and success from `auto` (unverified, P3 of D-009).
- **Consequence.** Under D-008, no builder session can start a successor run (§2.1), a review or verifier session (§5, PC-05) or, through a dispatcher, a run (§2.3). This run therefore cannot start the W-C00-12.6 verifier, any tranche 1c session (its Verifier, live tests or probes) or its own successor; an S4 hand-over is impossible from it. D-008 (Batu's decision on the permission mode of the sessions on his account) as decided conflicts with the need of the work, so the choice goes to him (operating model §6, Precedence; Appendix E §3): **D-009**, options (a) one starter session in auto mode, recommended, and (b) D-008 kept whole. Not offered: an account setting that removes the approval (it would contain the refused outcome) and in-process reviewers in place of review sessions (a reduced requirement). It goes to issue #6 as a comment that mentions him (§6), right after this record merges.
- **Observation for R-C00-BOM-5 R-4 (documented, not observed until now).** The guard decided the 22 tool calls of the researcher subagent (records carry its `agent_id`); the harness reported no effort for them. So hooks do run for in-process subagent calls in this session.
- **Plan from here (one sentence of reason).** The run continues with the work that needs no new session: `main` merged into PR #86 and 1c ported onto the D-008 guard with its tests, because that merge is needed whatever D-009's answer is; then it stops at S2 (the only remaining items need a session start, which waits for D-009).
- **Guard report** at 09:48Z: 1 denied (#27, L-118), 148 allowed, 0 passed to the user, hash chain intact.
- **Discipline line (D1–D9):** D1 the premise of D-008's P4 ("a PreToolUse allow skips the prompt") is false for session starts, observed and documented; D2 the parent's account is labelled as such; D4 the diagnostic call was chosen to separate two readings and was made once; D5 documented and observed facts are marked; D6 cause: a platform rule outside the guard's reach, not a guard gap; D9 library not consulted (a platform fact; the documentation is the source).
- **Record changes:** plan/decisions/D-009.md · addition · Batu's decision on starting sessions under D-008; plan/ledger.md summary_tr · supersession · the summary for Batu with D-009

### L-133 · 2026-10-05 · R-TRANS-1 round 1 FAIL; its three blocking findings and the minors fixed in the bundle
